        toppingCosts *= 2

        @cache

            """Return sum of subsequence of toppingCosts[i:] closest to x."""
            if x < 0 or i = len(toppingCosts+1: return 0

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.

Simplest:
```
class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        ans = min(baseCosts, key=lambda x: (abs(x-target), x))
        for base in baseCosts:
            def dfs(i, cur):
                nonlocal best
                total = base + cur
                if abs(total-target) < abs(best-target) or (abs(total-target)==abs(best-target) and total < best):
                    best = total
                if i == len(toppingCosts):
                    return
                for k in range(3):
                    dfs(i+1, cur + k*toppingCosts[i])
            best = base
            dfs(0,0)
            ans = min(ans, best, key=lambda x: (abs(x-target), x))
        return ans
```

Let's produce final code:
```
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            if i == len(toppingCosts) or x <= 0:
                return 0
            best = dfs(i + 1, x)
            for k in (1, 2):
                cand = k * toppingCosts[i] + dfs(i + 1, x - k * toppingCosts[i])
                if abs(cand - x) < abs(best - x) or (abs(cand - x) == abs(best - x) and cand < best):
                    best = cand
            return best

        ans = inf
        for base in baseCosts:
            top = dfs(0, target - base)
            total = base + top
            if abs(total - target) < abs(ans - target) or (abs(total - target) == abs(ans - target) and total < ans):
                ans = total
        return ans
```
Need `inf` defined? In Python, `inf` not built-in unless from math import inf. Could use `float('inf')`. Need include `from math import inf` or use `ans = None`. Better use `ans = float('inf')`. But type? `float('inf')` okay. Or `ans = min(baseCosts, key=...)` but if baseCosts non-empty (constraints yes). Use `ans = float('inf')`.

Need include `List` import. If LeetCode already imports, but okay. Could avoid type annotation? The method signature uses List, so need import. We can include `from typing import List`. If environment already has, no harm. Need output only code. Could include `from functools import lru_cache`.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            if i == len(toppingCosts) or x <= 0:
                return 0

            best = dfs(i + 1, x)
            for k in (1, 2):
                cand = k * toppingCosts[i] + dfs(i + 1, x - k * toppingCosts[i])
                if abs(cand - x) < abs(best - x) or (abs(cand - x) == abs(best - x) and cand < best):
                    best = cand
            return best

        ans = float('inf')
        for base in baseCosts:
            total = base + dfs(0, target - base)
            if abs(total - target) < abs(ans - target) or (abs(total - target) == abs(ans - target) and total < ans):
                ans = total

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            if i == len(toppingCosts) or x <= 0:
                return 0

            best = dfs(i + 1, x)
            for k in (1, 2):
                cand = k * toppingCosts[i] + dfs(i + 1, x - k * toppingCosts[i])
                if abs(cand - x) < abs(best - x) or (abs(cand - x) == abs(best - x) and cand < best):
                    best = cand
            return best

        ans = float('inf')
        for base in baseCosts:
            total = base + dfs(0, target - base)
            if abs(total - target) < abs(ans - target) or (abs(total - target) == abs(ans - target) and total < ans):
                ans = total

        return ans
